first stage = W ~ instrument + controls; report first-stage F, weak-instrument sensitivity), and the within-CEE subset spec.\\n\\nPHASE 7 - PRE-REGISTRATION CONTENT BLOCK (35 min) [D5; mostly writing, light verification]\\n- Primary-specification hierarchy: state that Design 1 as specified in 5c is the primary specification (exact variable definitions: outcome v2x_libdem from OWID; treatment WID pre-tax top-10% income share lagged t-3; W = WHO domestic general government health expenditure %GDP + World Bank EdStats education expenditure %GDP at earliest documented 3-year window in years 0-8, time-invariant thereafter; W x t, W x t^2; GDP per capita lagged; region x t; EU-accession dummy; country and year FE; country-clustered SEs; wild cluster bootstrap for the interaction marginal effects). List the order of checks exactly as they will be run.\\n- Decision rule (quote verbatim from the hypothesis and formalize): 'the gate holds if the interaction is significant at p<0.05 in the primary specification AND the marginal-effect pattern is confirmed in at least 5 of the 7 pre-specified robustness cells, with failures reported and interpreted.' Formalize the marginal-effect pattern: ME of INEQ on LDI is negative and significant at the 10th (and 25th) percentile of W; near zero or non-negative at the 75th/90th; and the 90th-minus-10th ME contrast is significant. State the disconfirmation conditions from the hypothesis flank (homogeneous effect; insignificant/wrong sign; collapse under W x t trends; reversed sequencing; placebo gating; dividend in thin-welfare countries).\\n- Exogeneity checks: (i) W regressed on first-decade inequality changes (change in top-10% share years 0-7) and fiscal-collapse indicators (peak annual CPI inflation in the first decade - source WB WDI/IMF IFS, verify OWID hosts inflation; cumulative real GDP change over the first 5 transition years - OWID GDP series), reporting coefficients, F-stats, partial R^2; (ii) placebo interactions (e.g., population age structure, from OWID) must NOT gate; (iii) 2SLS from Phase 6.\\n- Early-erosion rule: pre-commit - if ERT (or fallback threshold) codes erosion onset within the first 12 years after transition, the country is EXCLUDED from the primary sample and RECODED (included, with W at its earliest window and a flag) as robustness cell R5. State both arms explicitly so the next iteration codes them without discretion.\\n- MDES simulation design - write it fully as a runnable spec (equations + parameter grid; the next iteration codes it): sample N in {25, 30, 35} countries; T = 33 years (1990-2022) plus the effective T of each design window; DGP: y_it = a_i + t_t + b1*X_it + b2*(X_it x W_i) + u_it, u_it = rho*u_i(t-1) + e_it, e_it ~ N(0, sigma_e^2), rho in {0.7, 0.8, 0.9}; X_it (top-10% share) drawn as a country-specific AR(1) process with mean 40-50 and within-country SD ~2-3 percentage points (anchor to WID later; state the anchor rule); W_i standardized (and a median-split variant); noise calibrated so within-country LDI residual SD ~ 0.05-0.1 points (anchor: V-Dem LDI within-country variation in the roster; state the calibration rule); b1 anchored to the Rau-Stokes main-effect magnitude (VERIFY its point estimate in Phase 1-4 slack or note as 'to be anchored at estimation time'); b2 grid chosen so the gate implies the within-country inequality slope halves between the 10th and 90th W percentiles; estimator = the Design-1 spec exactly (FE + trends + clustered SE); inference = cluster-robust t-test and wild cluster bootstrap; 1000 simulations per cell; report power curves and the minimum detectable (i) b2, (ii) marginal-effect contrast in LDI points, at alpha 0.05, power 0.8, for each (N, rho) cell; pre-print the effective estimation sample size for each of the six designs and the listwise-deletion consequences below ~25 countries (e.g., 'if the effective sample falls below 25, the primary spec is underpowered and the paper reports the MDES table alongside' - pre-commit the response).\\n\\nPHASE 8 - REGISTRY PLAN AND ASSEMBLY (30 min) [D6 + outputs]\\n- Registry research: fetch OSF (osf.io) pre-registration documentation - expected: free, timestamped, DOI minting at 10.17605/OSF.IO/[id], standard 'Pre-registration' template fields (hypothesis, design, sample, measures, analysis plan), embargo option until acceptance; and EGAP (egap.org) - expected: design-pre-registration program for experiments and observational designs, submissions via their portal/OSF-linked review, registration IDs; compare and recommend (expected recommendation: OSF primary archive with the full protocol PDF; optionally EGAP registration for the design-review credential). Verify current templates and any fee/eligibility details.\\n- Deposit checklist (write it as a verbatim-usable list): hypothesis statement + confirmation/disconfirmation criteria; measurement protocol (treatment, outcome, W definition with per-country calendar windows); fixed country roster + coverage matrix (country x variable x window); six design specs (Phase 5c); decision rules (Phase 7); MDES plan (Phase 7); exogeneity and instrument diagnostics (Phases 6-7); data provenance and code-availability statement (all OWID + disclosed BMR/RoW cross-check; Python/R stack per the Phase 5b pre-commitment); versioning and amendment policy.\\n- Registry-ID citation formats (provide examples): OSF - 'Author(s), YYYY. Title. OSF Pre-registration. https://doi.org/10.17605/OSF.IO/XXXX'; EGAP - 'Author(s), YYYY. Title. EGAP Design Registration ID XXXXX. [URL]'; note the journal-agnostic convention that the paper cites the registration ID in the methods section.\\n- ASSEMBLY: write research_report.md with sections mirroring D1-D6 plus an executive summary, the feasibility decision tree, the risk/fallback log, and a 'next iteration needs' note (what the coder needs). Write research_out.json with this shape: {artifact: research_iter2_dir3, briefing: {prior_conditioning: [{paper_key, bibtex, core_result, differentiation}] , ANRR_verification: {claims: [{claim, quote, location, version}], correction_note}, design_specs: {design_1..design_6: {spec, variables, inference, package_path, notes}}, python_feasibility: {pyfixest_sunab: {available, version, api}, cs_path: {primary, fallback, rationale}, wild_bootstrap: {tool, rationale}, licensing_notes}, instruments: {communist_era: {...}, colonial_era: {...}, within_cee: {...}, feasibility_matrix}, preregistration_block: {hierarchy, decision_rule, marginal_effect_pattern, exogeneity_checks, early_erosion_rule, mdes: {dgp, grid, thresholds, outputs}}, registry_plan: {osf, egap, recommendation, deposit_checklist, citation_formats}}, sources: [{key, bibtex_ready, doi, url, accessed, verified_expected_value}], follow_up_questions: [...], risks_and_fallbacks: [...] }.\\n- End with SELF-CHECK against the direction's six deliverables and the hypothesis's success criteria; list anything unverified and its status. Add 3-5 sharp follow_up_questions for the strategy loop (e.g., whether the transfer-phacebo cell should count toward the 5-of-7 rule or only as a disconfirmation check; whether ERT onset or the threshold rule should be primary for erosion dating; whether the primary sample is the 1989-1998 cohort only or includes all democracies for Design 2; how to handle countries with no market-Gini data in the stabilization mechanism arm).\\n\\nRISK TABLE (plan-level; report where each landed)\\n- Paywalled primary sources (Cambridge Core, Project MUSE, Elsevier): fallback = author pages, NBER, arXiv, working-paper versions; flag any bibliometric detail verified only via secondary pages.\\n- ANRR chapter vs NBER pagination mismatch: report table numbers for BOTH versions.\\n- pyfixest sunab/did2s absent or API-changed at execution time: the plan's decision tree names the R via subprocess fallback; the next iteration's coder re-verifies the installed version before writing code.\\n- No Python CS package: pre-committed to R 'did' via subprocess/rpy2 with the manual CS spec written out as the fallback; do not leave it open.\\n- OWID availability gaps (market Gini, educated-youth unemployment, immunization): verify presence now; if absent, record the alternative placement (WID pre-tax Gini; ILO via OWID youth unemployment; UNICEF/WHO immunization on OWID) so the next iteration is not surprised.\\n- Time overrun: trimming order defined in the working rules; never skip D1-D4 and the design equations.\"\n}", "new_content": "{\n  \"title\": \"Welfare-gate pre-registration blueprint briefing\",\n  \"summary\": \"Plan for a 3-hour scholarly web-research artifact that produces the registration-ready estimation blueprint for the welfare-gate hypothesis: verified citations and one-sentence differentiations for Svolik 2015, Huber-Stephens 2001/2012, Rothstein-Uslaner 2005; correction of the ANRR 2015 top-income-share vs Gini characterization with table-level evidence; exact estimator specifications for the six designs with a Python feasibility decision tree (pyfixest sunab, Callaway-Sant'Anna via R/rpy2 or documented manual implementation, wild cluster bootstrap); 2SLS legacy-instrument data sources and roster feasibility; the full pre-registration content block including the minimum-detectable-effect simulation spec; and the OSF/EGAP registry plan with deposit checklist. Output: research_out.json + research_report.md, BibTeX-ready, structured so GEN_PAPER_TEXT can cite directly.\",\n  \"runpod_compute_profile\": \"cpu_light\",\n  \"question\": \"What are the exact, verified citations; the corrected account of what Acemoglu-Naidu-Restrepo-Robinson 2015 actually report on top-income shares versus the Gini; the precise estimator specifications for six pre-registered designs (trend-controlled two-way fixed effects with W x t and W x t-squared, Callaway-Sant'Anna and Sun-Abraham staggered event studies, Goodman-Bacon diagnostics, wild cluster bootstrap); the concrete data sources and roster feasibility for communist-era and colonial-era legacy instruments; the complete pre-registration content (primary-specification hierarchy, 5-of-7 decision rule, exogeneity checks, early-erosion exclusion rule, minimum-detectable-effect simulation DGP with N=25-35 and rho 0.7-0.9); and the OSF/EGAP registry deposit plan \u2014 that a research executor must gather so the paper's Sections 2, 5, and 6 can be rewritten as a deposit-ready protocol?\",\n  \"explanation\": \"This artifact matters because the hypothesis is explicitly a pre-registered prediction with no model estimated yet, and the reviewer (an Acemoglu-calibrated reader) demanded exactly three things before any estimation: honest engagement with the closest prior work, estimator specifications precise enough to run without further decisions, and a protocol complete enough to deposit in a timestamped registry. Everything downstream depends on this briefing: GEN_PAPER_TEXT will cite these verified entries verbatim, and the next iteration's executor will code the six designs and the MDES simulation directly from these spec blocks. If the citations are wrong (e.g., the ANRR Gini-vs-top-share claim misstated), the paper embeds an error; if the Python package feasibility is unverified (e.g., assuming a Callaway-Sant'Anna implementation that does not exist), the next iteration wastes a full cycle. The research executor is the only agent that can resolve these factual questions by reading the primary sources, so this plan is built to make that reading fast, complete, and recorded.\",\n  \"research_plan\": \"GENERAL WORKING RULES\\n- Tools: use the aii-web-tools skill exactly as documented - built-in WebSearch/WebFetch if present, otherwise the skill scripts (set PY per the skill; search --query ... --mode scholarly for DOI/bibliographic lookups via OpenAlex+Crossref, --mode general for discovering PDF copies; fetch for reading; grep with -i for exact claims, numbers, and table references inside long PDFs). For the heavy multi-source passes (Phase 1-4 citation verification, Phase 5 package feasibility), optionally invoke aii-web-research-tools before starting to run a structured campaign. Parallelize aggressively: all searches within a phase are independent and can go in one turn; only fetch after the search that produced the URL.\\n- Time budget: 3 hours total. Hard allocation: Phase 1 Svolik 30 min; Phase 2 Rothstein-Uslaner 25 min; Phase 3 Huber-Stephens 25 min; Phase 4 ANRR 40 min; Phase 5 estimator specs + Python feasibility 45 min; Phase 6 instruments 30 min; Phase 7 pre-registration block 35 min; Phase 8 registry + assembly 30 min. If behind schedule, trim in this order: registry details (keep only the checklist and citation format), Phase 6 non-essential instrument sources, Phase 5b package-history digressions. NEVER trim Phases 1-4 and the design-spec equations.\\n- Spend: zero LLM calls. The web stack is free-first; do not fall back to paid Serper unless a search genuinely fails and the query is load-bearing.\\n- Veracity protocol: every bibliographic fact below is marked with an expected value from the planner's prior knowledge. Treat every expected value as UNVERIFIED until you confirm it on a fetched page (journal landing page, DOI resolver, publisher page, or PDF first page). Record citation metadata in a running log (author, year, title, venue, vol(issue):pages, DOI, URL, date accessed) as you go.\\n- Deliverables: research_report.md (prose briefing, structured as below) and research_out.json {answer, sources, follow_up_questions} - suggested JSON shape in Phase 8.\\n\\nPHASE 0 - SETUP AND ORIENTATION (10 min)\\n- Read the artifact direction and the hypothesis once more; freeze the six deliverables as your checklist: (D1) prior-conditioning briefing; (D2) ANRR 2015 verification; (D3) estimator specs for six designs incl. Python feasibility decision; (D4) 2SLS legacy-instrument design and roster feasibility; (D5) pre-registration content block incl. MDES; (D6) registry plan.\\n- Create your work directory under the artifact workspace (subdirectory named sources/ for saved notes if the environment allows file writes; otherwise keep notes in the running log).\\n- Set a phase timer log; record end-of-phase timestamps.\\n\\nPHASE 1 - PRIOR CONDITIONING: SVOLIK 2015 (30 min) [DELIVERABLE D1, first paper]\\n- Search (scholarly): Svolik \\\"Which Democracies Will Last?\\\" British Journal of Political Science; also general search for a free PDF (Svolik's academic page, campuspress/Cambridge Core, ResearchGate).\\n- Expected values to VERIFY: Milan W. Svolik, \\\"Which Democracies Will Last? Coups, Incumbent Takeovers, and the Dynamic of Democratic Consolidation,\\\" British Journal of Political Science 45(4):715-738 (2015), DOI 10.1017/S0007123413000550; earlier FirstView 2014.\\n- Fetch the abstract and, if reachable, the PDF. Extract and quote (with page numbers) the following, which the briefing must state exactly: (1) the two breakdown modes - coups vs incumbent takeovers - and their incidence; (2) the central survival-result: the hazard of democratic breakdown falls with democratic age, but sharply differently for the two modes (expected: coup hazard declines fast and is concentrated in young democracies; incumbent-takeover hazard declines slowly, so after roughly 15-25 years incumbent takeovers dominate - find his exact numbers, e.g., survival probabilities or hazard ratio estimates at given ages); (3) which covariates his hazard models include (expected: democratic age, income/GDP per capita, and possibly a test with inequality/income distribution - VERIFY whether inequality appears; this determines how you phrase the differentiation); (4) his data and sample (expected: Boix-Miller-Rosato type regime data, 1800s-2000s, ~200-400 democracies).\\n- Failure fallbacks: if the PDF is paywalled, the abstract plus precise secondary characterizations (his own book 'The Politics of Authoritarian Rule' summarizes it; reviews cite the numbers) - but you MUST then flag in the briefing which numbers are abstract-verified vs PDF-verified.\\n- Output for D1: a 200-300 word briefing block with (a) full BibTeX-ready entry; (b) core result in 2-3 sentences with quoted numbers; (c) one-sentence differentiation, calibrated to the following claim (adjust only if evidence demands): \\\"Svolik (2015) defines the population at risk - young democracies' distinct vulnerability to incumbent takeover - but his hazard models condition on democratic age and income, not on welfare institutions, and test no inequality-shock transmission structure; the welfare gate adds the institutional conditioning of the inequality-erosion link within that at-risk population.\\\"\\n\\nPHASE 2 - PRIOR CONDITIONING: ROTHSTEIN AND USLANER 2005 (25 min) [D1, second paper]\\n- Search (scholarly): Rothstein Uslaner \\\"All for All: Equality, Corruption, and Social Trust\\\" World Politics 2005; general search for free PDF (Uslaner's university page, QoG Working Paper version 2003).\n- Expected values to VERIFY: Bo Rothstein and Eric M. Uslaner, World Politics 58(1):41-72 (October 2005), DOI 10.1353/wp.2006.0022.\\n- Extract with quotes: (1) the causal chain universal welfare programs -> economic equality + low corruption -> generalized social trust -> institutional legitimacy; (2) the contrast claim that targeted/means-tested programs breed distrust, clientelism, and lower legitimacy; (3) the 'Big Bang' claim that trust levels change only through dramatic policy shifts; (4) their empirical strategy (World Values Survey cross-country and US cross-state evidence), so the briefing can note the design gap (cross-sectional survey correlations, advanced democracies - no dynamic panel of young democracies, no inequality-shock x welfare interaction).\\n- Output: full BibTeX entry (World Politics, DOI above), core result, one-sentence differentiation: \\\"Rothstein and Uslaner (2005) supply the microfoundation - universalism raises equality and trust, which underwrite institutional legitimacy - but their evidence is cross-sectional survey correlations in settled democracies, with no shock-transmission structure; the gate converts their static microfoundation into a dynamic conditioning hypothesis about why inequality shocks erode liberal-democratic quality only where universal services are thin.\\\"\\n\\nPHASE 3 - PRIOR CONDITIONING: HUBER AND STEPHENS 2001/2012 (25 min) [D1, third paper]\\n- Search: Huber Stephens \\\"Development and Crisis of the Welfare State\\\" 2001 University of Chicago Press; Huber Stephens \\\"Democracy and the Left\\\" 2012 social policy inequality Latin America. Also fetch publisher/ISBN pages and substantive reviews (or chapter PDFs if reachable) to ground the two core claims.\\n- Expected values to VERIFY: (1) Evelyne Huber and John D. Stephens, Development and Crisis of the Welfare State: Parties and Policies in Global Markets, University of Chicago Press, 2001 (ISBNs exist for cloth/paper; capture them); core: welfare-state effort and structure are shaped by partisan composition of government, power resources, and historical legacies, and globalization did not uniformly force retrenchment; the book's Latin American chapters relate welfare state development to regime dynamics. (2) Evelyne Huber and John D. Stephens, Democracy and the Left: Social Policy and Inequality in Latin America, University of Chicago Press, 2012; core: in Latin America, democracy combined with left governments expanded social policy (health, education, transfers) and reduced inequality, demonstrating partisanship as the proximate driver of social investment.\\n- The briefing must verify the exact wording of the claim the hypothesis attributes to them ('welfare states underwrite democratic legitimacy and consolidation in Latin America') - find where H&S make the legitimacy/consolidation link (2001 conclusions; 2012 discussion) and quote it.\\n- Output: both BibTeX entries; core results; differentiation sentence: \\\"Huber and Stephens (2001, 2012) establish the constituency logic - welfare states, particularly universalistic social policy, underwrite democratic legitimacy and in Latin America inequality fell where democracy and left governments expanded social policy - but their causal variables are partisanship and power resources, their designs are pooled time-series without an inequality-shock moderation test, and they do not estimate the gate: whether pre-determined universal services condition the transmission of market-inequality surges into graded liberal-democratic erosion across the post-1990 cohort.\\\"\\n\\nPHASE 4 - ANRR 2015 VERIFICATION (40 min) [D2; the most fact-sensitive deliverable]\\n- Locate the text: NBER Working Paper 19746 'Democracy, Redistribution and Inequality' (Acemoglu, Naidu, Restrepo, Robinson; free PDF at nber.org/papers/w19746) and/or the Handbook chapter PDF on Acemoglu's MIT page. The published version: Handbook of Income Distribution, Vol. 2, eds. A.B. Atkinson and F. Bourguignon, Elsevier 2015, pages 1885-1966 (expected DOI 10.1016/B978-0-444-59429-7.00022-4 - VERIFY on the DOI landing page). Elsevier is paywalled; the NBER version is the working text - state in the briefing which version each table reference comes from (page/table numbers differ between NBER WP and Handbook chapter; record both if possible).\\n- Grep the PDF (case-insensitive) for: 'Gini', 'top decile', 'top 10', 'top income', 'inequality', 'Table 5', 'Table 6', 'Table 7', 'schooling', 'tax revenue', 'precise', 'imprecise', 'not significant'.\\n- Extract, with exact table/figure numbers and the numbers themselves: (1) the schooling result (expected: democratization raises enrollment/schooling robustly - get the point estimates and significance); (2) the taxation result (tax revenue/GDP rises after democratization - get estimates); (3) the inequality results - CRITICAL: confirm that the effect on top income shares (expected: top decile share falls by roughly 2-3 percentage points, significant) is the headline inequality result, and that the Gini results are imprecise/insignificant (expected: negative point estimates around -0.9 to -1.5 Gini points, not significant) - quote the abstract sentence and the table; (4) which inequality data they use (expected: WID / Alvaredo-Atkinson-Piketty-Saez top shares, and a Gini source such as WIID or Deininger-Squire); (5) the precise claims about the 'democratic dividend' (democracy -> redistribution/taxation/schooling) and any short-run growth finding.\\n- Adjudicate the hypothesis's characterization: the hypothesis states the dividend 'is stated on top-income shares, which is what that work actually reports, not on the Gini'. Confirm or correct that phrasing based on what you find, and produce a short 'correction note' listing any nuance (e.g., if the chapter reports both but emphasizes top shares; if 'top decile' rather than 'top 10 percent' is the exact language; whether effects are in percentage points of the income share).\\n- Output: a verification block with (a) full BibTeX for both NBER WP and Handbook chapter; (b) a claims table: claim | verified quote | table/fig/page | source version (NBER vs Handbook); (c) the correction note; (d) implications for the hypothesis's dividend design (Design 4 must be stated on top-income shares, dividend window years 0-7).\\n\\nPHASE 5 - ESTIMATOR SPECIFICATIONS AND PYTHON FEASIBILITY (45 min) [D3]\\n5a. Cite and pin the estimators (20 min):\\n- Callaway and Sant'Anna 2021, 'Difference-in-Differences with Multiple Time Periods,' Journal of Econometrics 225(2):200-230 (expected DOI 10.1016/j.jeconom.2020.12.001; arXiv 1803.09015). Verify volume/issue/pages/DOI; fetch the arXiv abstract; note the estimator structure (group-time ATT(g,t), never-treated and not-yet-treated comparison groups, doubly robust estimation combining outcome regression and propensity score, pre-treatment parallel-trends tests, aggregation schemes) and the software: R package 'did' (bcallaway11.github.io/did; met for Callaway-Sant'Anna), Stata 'csdid' (Rios-Avila/Fernando Rios-Avila).\\n- Sun and Abraham 2021, 'Estimating Dynamic Treatment Effects in Event Studies with Heterogeneous Treatment Effects,' Journal of Econometrics 225(2):175-199 (expected DOI 10.1016/j.jeconom.2020.09.006; arXiv 2004.13794). Verify metadata; extract the core problem (TWFE leads/lags are contaminated under heterogeneous and dynamic effects) and the fix (interaction-weighted estimator: saturated cohort-by-relative-time regression aggregated with cohort-size weights) and their pre-trend test.\\n- Goodman-Bacon 2021, 'Difference-in-Differences with Variation in Treatment Timing,' Journal of Econometrics 225(2):254-277 (expected DOI 10.1016/j.jeconom.2021.03.014): the decomposition of TWFE DiD into 2x2 comparisons and the negative-weight problem; software: Stata 'bacondecomp', R 'bacondecomp'.\\n- Note that all three are in the same JoE 225(2) (Dec 2021) special issue - a nice citation detail.\\n5b. Python feasibility decision tree (25 min) - the executor's job here is DOCUMENTARY (no code can run):\\n- Fetch pyfixest docs (pyfixest.readthedocs.io): find the current version; confirm the existence, version, and API of the staggered-DiD functions - expected: sunab() implementing Sun-Abraham, did2s() implementing Gardner's two-stage estimator - and whether anything implements Callaway-Sant'Anna or a Goodman-Bacon decomposition; also check supported inference (heteroskedasticity-robust, CRVE; whether a wild cluster bootstrap option exists). Record the exact function signature/sample usage from the docs so the next iteration can code directly.\\n- Search PyPI/GitHub: 'callaway sant anna python', 'csdid pypi', 'python staggered did package', 'python difference in differences multiple time periods', 'python bacon decomposition did'. Expected finding: no mature, maintained Python package implements Callaway-Sant'Anna; verify or refute and record what exists. Also check 'didimputation' (Borusyak-Kuang-Morris) and 'event_study'/'staggered' ports if they surface.\\n- Check the R-path feasibility: rpy2 on PyPI and its compatibility notes, plus the R 'did' package CRAN page and its dependency/version requirements, because the committed CS path will either be (i) R 'did' invoked via rpy2 or an Rscript subprocess, or (ii) a documented manual implementation (the no-covariate version of ATT(g,t) reduces to 2x2 DiD means aggregated per CS formulas; the covariate version uses doubly robust estimation implementable with statsmodels logistic + OLS).\\n- Check wild-cluster-bootstrap options: search 'wild cluster bootstrap python', 'fwildclusterboot R', 'boottest Stata Roodman'. Expected: R 'fwildclusterboot' (and Stata 'boottest', Roodman et al. 2019 Stata Journal 19(1):4-60) are the mature tools; any Python implementation (e.g., within pyfixest or a standalone package) - verify existence and maturity. Reference the canonical citation: Cameron, Gelbach, Miller 2008, Review of Economics and Statistics 90(3):414-427, DOI 10.1162/rest.90.3.414.\\n- WRITE THE PRE-COMMITMENT (this is what the reviewer demanded): a decision tree stating the PRIMARY path (expected: pyfixest sunab for Sun-Abraham; Callaway-Sant'Anna via the R 'did' package through subprocess/rpy2 if R is available in the next iteration's environment, otherwise the documented manual CS implementation; Goodman-Bacon diagnostics via a Python port if one exists, else the R 'bacondecomp' via subprocess; wild cluster bootstrap via R 'fwildclusterboot' or a documented manual Rademacher implementation) and the FALLBACK for each node, with a one-line rationale. State explicitly that this pre-commitment fixes the software stack BEFORE estimation, so the pre-registration names the estimator and package for every design.\\n5c. Write the six design spec blocks (synthesis; no new search needed; these are equations - present them as estimable specifications with variables, lags, fixed effects, clustering, and inference for each):\\n- Design 1 (gate, primary): LDI_it = a_i + t_t + b1*INEQ_i(t-3) + b2*(INEQ_i(t-3) x W_i) + W_i x t + W_i x t^2 + g*GDPpc_i(t-1) + region x t + EU_access_it + e_it; country and year FE; SEs clustered by country; W fixed at earliest 3-year window within years 0-8 of the first democratic decade (calendar years documented per country); INEQ = WID pre-tax top-10% income share (primary), market Gini (secondary); marginal effects of INEQ at the 10th/25th/50th/75th/90th percentiles of W with wild cluster bootstrap over the interaction; five-year-differences variant (regress LDI_it - LDI_i(t-5) on lagged inequality change x W with the same FE/trend structure); Goodman-Bacon decomposition and (if available) the CS/SA robustness on the same sample.\\n- Design 2 (adjudication): (i) baseline main effect of INEQ without interaction; (ii) with interaction; run on (a) full democracy sample and (b) young-democracy sample; expected pattern stated from the hypothesis (weak main effect, strong interaction).\\n- Design 3 (sequencing): staggered erosion-onset event studies. Pre-commit the erosion-onset definition: first year of an autocratization episode from V-Dem's Episodes of Regime Transformation (ERT) dataset - verify ERT availability and version (search 'Episodes of Regime Transformation V-Dem'; expected: V-Dem Institute/GitHub, versions v12-v15; cite Maerz et al. and Luhrmann-Lindberg 2019 Democratization 26(7):1095-1113 'A third wave of autocratization is here') - with a fallback threshold rule (first year of a sustained >=0.03-point 3-year decline in v2x_libdem) pre-committed; outcomes traced around onset: LDI, top-10% share, health+education spending %GDP; separate estimates by welfare arm (median split); CS group-time ATTs and SA interaction-weighted coefficients; pre-trend tests (joint significance of leads); report the lead-lag structure (does service decline precede LDI decline by 3-5 years in the thin arm; is there NO anticipatory service decline in the high arm).\\n- Design 4 (democratization dividend): event study around transition year T0 (first democratic year, RoW/BMR dating); outcomes: top-10% share and public services over years -5..+12 by welfare legacy; dividend = mean change years 0-7 in top-10% share; second stage: 2010-2022 LDI change regressed on first-decade dividend size (with the W x t controls). Stated on top-income shares per Phase 4.\\n- Design 5 (mechanisms): (i) stabilization - add the market-minus-disposable Gini gap (lagged) as control: prediction that the gate attenuates once the gap is controlled; (ii) constituency - interact INEQ with composition measures (universal services vs targeted transfers share; middle-class-usage proxies such as immunization coverage and primary enrollment rates - verify OWID hosts them); (iii) pre-commitment - program automaticity proxy (share of automatic/entitlement-based spending; document data feasibility and expected sign); (iv) polarization arm (V-Dem v2x_polarization; verify variable name on OWID) reported as mechanism report only; (v) mismatch arm - ILO educated-youth unemployment interaction (verify OWID hosts 'unemployment by education level' or similar; expected: OWID youth unemployment series exist, education-disaggregated availability questionable - document and propose fallback).\\n- Design 6 (robustness: pre-commit the exact 7 cells that feed the 5-of-7 rule): R1 alternative inequality measure (market Gini for primary top-10); R2 alternative welfare measure/window (education-only and health-only splits; W at the T+8..12 window sensitivity pair); R3 population-weighted regression; R4 commodity-boom exclusion (drop/flag major fuel-exporting democratizers 2000-2014 or add commodity-price control - pre-commit one); R5 early-erosion exclusion/recoding (see Phase 7 rule); R6 within-Eastern-Europe estimate; R7 full-sample transfer placebo (ILO WSPR or IMF GFS total social expenditure as moderator in place of W - must NOT gate). Diagnostics (exogeneity regressions, 2SLS) are reported outside the 7-cell rule.\\n\\nPHASE 6 - 2SLS LEGACY INSTRUMENTS (30 min) [D4]\\n- Instrument 1 - communist-era schooling enrollment (serves Eastern Europe + Mongolia arm): expected sources: Barro-Lee educational attainment dataset (barrolee.com; BL2018 or BL2025, 5-year intervals from 1950; use 1970/1980 enrollment or attainment for CEE countries; canonical citation Barro and Lee 2013, Journal of Applied Econometrics 28(6):906-931, DOI 10.1002/jae.2313 - VERIFY); UNICEF TransMONEE database (28 transition countries; verify start years - expected late 1980s/1989 onward, so document that pre-transition values come mainly from Barro-Lee/UNESCO historical yearbooks); UNESCO UIS historical series if findable. Verify availability of 1970-1980 schooling enrollment for the CEE/CIS roster (Poland, Hungary, Czech/Slovak, Slovenia, Croatia, Bulgaria, Romania, Estonia/Latvia/Lithuania, Albania, Macedonia, Moldova, Mongolia).\\n- Instrument 2 - colonial-era public-service coverage (serves African and Latin American arms): expected sources: Huillery 2009 'History matters: The long-term impact of colonial public investments in French West Africa,' Journal of Development Economics (VERIFY volume/pages/DOI - expect vol 90, 2009) - colonial education/health investment measures for Benin, Mali, etc.; Frankema 2012 'The origins of formal education in sub-Saharan Africa: was British rule more benign?,' European Review of Economic History 16(4) (VERIFY) for colonial schooling breadth; optionally colonial railways/missionary presence (Nunn 2008/2010 strand) for robustness instruments. document the selection procedure and mark each country by which instrument arm covers it (communist-legacy vs colonial-legacy vs neither) and the coverage years available.\\n- Within-Eastern-Europe variant: restrict to former communist countries; W instrumented by communist-era schooling enrollment (1980, Barro-Lee) + the Design-1 controls; note the identification gain (communist legacy held constant by sample construction) and the cost (N ~ 10-15); pre-commit this as a confirmatory-but-underpowered cell, not part of the 5-of-7 rule.\\n- Output: instrument table (instrument | data source | coverage years | roster coverage | first-stage expectation | exclusion-restriction note), the 2SLS specification (second stage = Design 1 with W instrumented; first stage = W ~ instrument + controls; report first-stage F, weak-instrument sensitivity), and the within-CEE subset spec.\\n\\nPHASE 7 - PRE-REGISTRATION CONTENT BLOCK (35 min) [D5; mostly writing, light verification]\\n- Primary-specification hierarchy: state that Design 1 as specified in 5c is the primary specification (exact variable definitions: outcome v2x_libdem from OWID; treatment WID pre-tax top-10% income share lagged t-3; W = WHO domestic general government health expenditure %GDP + World Bank EdStats education expenditure %GDP at earliest documented 3-year window in years 0-8, time-invariant thereafter; W x t, W x t^2; GDP per capita lagged; region x t; EU-accession dummy; country and year FE; country-clustered SEs; wild cluster bootstrap for the interaction marginal effects). List the order of checks exactly as they will be run.\\n- Decision rule (quote verbatim from the hypothesis and formalize): 'the gate holds if the interaction is significant at p<0.05 in the primary specification AND the marginal-effect pattern is confirmed in at least 5 of the 7 pre-specified robustness cells, with failures reported and interpreted.' Formalize the marginal-effect pattern: ME of INEQ on LDI is negative and significant at the 10th (and 25th) percentile of W; near zero or non-negative at the 75th/90th; and the 90th-minus-10th ME contrast is significant. State the disconfirmation conditions from the hypothesis flank (homogeneous effect; insignificant/wrong sign; collapse under W x t trends; reversed sequencing; placebo gating; dividend in thin-welfare countries).\\n- Exogeneity checks: (i) W regressed on first-decade inequality changes (change in top-10% share years 0-7) and fiscal-collapse indicators (peak annual CPI inflation in the first decade - source WB WDI/IMF IFS, verify OWID hosts inflation; cumulative real GDP change over the first 5 transition years - OWID GDP series), reporting coefficients, F-stats, partial R^2; (ii) placebo interactions (e.g., population age structure, from OWID) must NOT gate; (iii) 2SLS from Phase 6.\\n- Early-erosion rule: pre-commit - if ERT (or fallback threshold) codes erosion onset within the first 12 years after transition, the country is EXCLUDED from the primary sample and RECODED (included, with W at its earliest window and a flag) as robustness cell R5. State both arms explicitly so the next iteration codes them without discretion.\\n- MDES simulation design - write it fully as a runnable spec (equations + parameter grid; the next iteration codes it): sample N in {25, 30, 35} countries; T = 33 years (1990-2022) plus the effective T of each design window; DGP: y_it = a_i + t_t + b1*X_it + b2*(X_it x W_i) + u_it, u_it = rho*u_i(t-1) + e_it, e_it ~ N(0, sigma_e^2), rho in {0.7, 0.8, 0.9}; X_it (top-10% share) drawn as a country-specific AR(1) process with mean 40-50 and within-country SD ~2-3 percentage points (anchor to WID later; state the anchor rule); W_i standardized (and a median-split variant); noise calibrated so within-country LDI residual SD ~ 0.05-0.1 points (anchor: V-Dem LDI within-country variation in the roster; state the calibration rule); b1 anchored to the Rau-Stokes main-effect magnitude (VERIFY its point estimate in Phase 1-4 slack or note as 'to be anchored at estimation time'); b2 grid chosen so the gate implies the within-country inequality slope halves between the 10th and 90th W percentiles; estimator = the Design-1 spec exactly (FE + trends + clustered SE); inference = cluster-robust t-test and wild cluster bootstrap; 1000 simulations per cell; report power curves and the minimum detectable (i) b2, (ii) marginal-effect contrast in LDI points, at alpha 0.05, power 0.8, for each (N, rho) cell; pre-print the effective estimation sample size for each of the six designs and the listwise-deletion consequences below ~25 countries (e.g., 'if the effective sample falls below 25, the primary spec is underpowered and the paper reports the MDES table alongside' - pre-commit the response).\\n\\nPHASE 8 - REGISTRY PLAN AND ASSEMBLY (30 min) [D6 + outputs]\\n- Registry research: fetch OSF (osf.io) pre-registration documentation - expected: free, timestamped, DOI minting at 10.17605/OSF.IO/[id], standard 'Pre-registration' template fields (hypothesis, design, sample, measures, analysis plan), embargo option until acceptance; optionally EGAP registration for the design-review credential). Verify current templates and any fee/eligibility details.\\n- Deposit checklist (write it as a verbatim-usable list): hypothesis statement + confirmation/disconfirmation criteria; measurement protocol (treatment, outcome, W definition with per-country calendar windows); fixed country roster + coverage matrix (country x variable x window); six design specs (Phase 5c); decision rules (Phase 7); MDES plan (Phase 7); exogeneity and instrument diagnostics (Phases 6-7); data provenance and code-availability statement (all OWID + disclosed BMR/RoW cross-check; Python/R stack per the Phase 5b pre-commitment); versioning and amendment policy.\\n- Registry-ID citation formats (provide examples): OSF - 'Author(s), YYYY. [URL]'; note the journal-agnostic convention that the paper cites the registration ID in the methods section.\\n- ASSEMBLY: write research_report.md with sections mirroring D1-D6 plus an executive summary, the feasibility decision tree, the risk/fallback log, and a 'next iteration needs' note (what the coder needs).
